# Implementation cycle — 050.1-the-claim

Pulled from EPIC: `.agents/plan/epics/050.1-the-claim.md`.

Verification Gate (binding, from the EPIC's `## Verification Gate` section):

> Gates: `pnpm run verify`
>
> Proof:
>
> ```bash
> node --test \
>   src/commands/run/expire-runs.test.ts \
>   src/commands/node/claim-node.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/errors.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/example.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/coverage.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/event-payload.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/graph.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/registry.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/openapi.test.ts \
>   src/http/contract/parity.test.ts \
>   test/helpers/sequence-conformance.test.ts \
>   test/sequence/conformance.test.ts \
>   scripts/verify-epic-sequence.test.ts \
>   && node scripts/verify-epic-sequence.ts \
>   && echo "PASS EPIC-050.1"
> ```
>
> Hermetic coverage required beyond the Proof:
>
> - `heartbeatIntervalMs` is absent from `nodeClaimResponse` and `renewAfterMs` equals `Math.floor(runTtlMs / 3)`, asserted by value. A claim's `expiresAt` equals its run row's `expires_at`. Story 1.
> - `lease.claimed` is absent from `eventTypes`, `run.opened` and `run.expired` are present, and `retiredEventTypes` is empty. All asserted, so the replacement is complete rather than additive. Story 1.
> - The expiry pass raises the fence by exactly one, and a second call raises nothing and appends no second event. Story 2.
> - A failure injected at the `run.expired` event append leaves the run `active` and the fence unchanged, proving the transition and the event are one transaction. Story 2.
> - The expiry pass receives the same transaction the command opened, asserted by identity, so `storage.transact` appearing once in the diagram is backed by a case. Story 2.
> - The claim opens exactly one run. A task claim is asserted to leave exactly one active run row, and no parent objective run. Story 3.
> - A second claim by the same worker on a node with an active run refuses `subtree-busy` with `relation: "self"`, proving the deleted replay path has a defined replacement. Story 3.
> - Every claim refusal this story raises leaves the database byte-identical, asserted for the eleven codes it can provoke. This is what the shipped ordering did not hold. The twelfth, `objective-busy`, is Story 5's. Story 3.
> - A claim on an unassigned node writes `node.assignment` and inserts the run. A test reads both rows after the transaction and asserts the worker id is equal in each. Story 3.
> - A claim on an assigned node by a different worker refuses `assignment-held`, and the details name the current assignment. Story 3.
> - The claim's refusal precedence is asserted by a decision table over every pair of the eleven refusals this story raises that can trigger at once, and the table asserts the winner for each pair. A trace proves the read order and never the precedence. Story 3.
> - A second active run on the same node is refused by the `run_one_active` index against real SQLite. The index case is the same-node case only, and the pure-function case is EPIC 050's. Story 3.
> - A claim on a descendant of a node holding an active run refuses, and the refusal names the ancestor node id. Story 3.
> - A claim carrying `available: false` refuses `unroutable` with `failedSet: "available"`, and no run row is written. Story 3.
> - A `review` claim refuses `review-head-unavailable`, and the refusal is asserted to sit between `unroutable` and `lease-held` in the decision table. Story 3.
> - `node.assignment` is unchanged after an expiry and after an unroutable claim on a different node. Story 3.
> - The ancestor cascade is unrolled and each step carries its node alias, so the cascade order is asserted. A cascade of zero fails. Story 3.
> - `initiative-not-claimable` and `run-driver-mismatch` are absent from `ClaimRefusal`, asserted by the type's member list, and a claim on an initiative whose deliverable is `expansion` succeeds. Story 4.
> - `claim-success-task` and `claim-success-initiative` are separate diagrams owned by separate stories, and the initiative trace holds no `execution.openAttempt` and no objective-scoped read. A structural run that opened an attempt therefore fails. Story 4.
> - A claim on a task refuses `objective-busy`, the refusal names the sibling node id, the sibling run id and its `expires_at`, and it leaves the database byte-identical. Story 5.
> - The decision table gains one row per refusal that can trigger beside `objective-busy`, each with its winner, and the extended table covers every pair exactly once. `assignment-held` beating `objective-busy` is one such row. Story 5.
> - `claim-refusal-objective-busy` records no write seam after the refusal point. That the operation committed nothing is a separate assertion of the bullet above, because the expiry pass mutates inside the same transaction and is hidden behind one step by design. Story 5.
> - The parser refuses every malformed diagram by value, including two steps carrying one token, the words `loop` and `opt`, and a `<key>.call` or `:#<n>` outside a `baseline-` id. Each refusal is asserted, so the gate cannot pass by parsing nothing. Story 6.
> - A recorded list with one extra step, one missing step, two adjacent steps swapped, and one differing label each fail. Without all four the comparison could be a subset check. Story 6.
> - Every diagram of every story of this epic is replayed against the real command over real SQLite, and the recorded token list equals the drawn list by `deepStrictEqual`. The diagram is the only copy of the expected order, so a diagram nobody updated fails with the implementation. Story 7.
> - Every baseline diagram holds no `test/sequence/scenarios/<id>.ts` file, and the range gate refuses one. A baseline is evidence, and it never runs. Story 8.
> - Every live diagram token that is no context token of its baseline is `+` or `~` in exactly one story, and every `-` token appears in that diagram's baseline or carries a `@<file>:<line>` citation. A removal the shipped code never made therefore fails the gate. Story 8.
> - Every story of this range owns at most one live diagram, asserted by the gate. A story owning two is too big, and a story that changes a shipped path and declares no baseline fails. Story 8.
> - No epic file of the range holds a mermaid block, asserted by the gate over `.agents/plan/epics/`. An epic states decisions and a gate, and it draws nothing. Story 8.
> - The run model document names every member of the exported claim refusal list, built by importing that list rather than restating twelve literals, and the count is asserted at twelve. Story 9.

TDD protocol:

1. test-engineer writes the next failing test (RED) — or a GREEN-ONLY pass-through for cases that state a build-only check.
2. software-engineer makes the test green (RED flow) or implements the story's `## Change` for the forwarded cases (GREEN-ONLY flow).
3. test-engineer confirms GREEN (or runs a build-only check for GREEN-ONLY), then either opens the next case or runs the full Verification Gate and appends IMPLEMENTATION_READY_FOR_REVIEW.

## HUMAN REVIEW — EPIC 050.1

**Date.** 2026-09-03. Base ref `3ab9b283753f4d2905e2be00fbf1ac5e08636263`. Mode: final.

**B7a — the two diagram corrections are confirmed.** Both were verified against the code and the
fixture, independently of the implementation that made them:

- `03-the-claim-of-a-task.md` step 21 `events.append:run.opened:T` to `:R`. The append at
  `src/commands/node/claim-node.ts:426-428` carries `subjectKind: "run"` and `subjectId: run.id`.
  An event whose subject kind is `run` cannot carry a node alias, so `:T` was wrong before the
  implementation existed.
- `04-the-claim-of-an-initiative.md` gained `7 lease.read:O`, and its prose moved from one relative
  to two. `relativesOf` at `src/commands/node/claim-node.ts:670-687` returns the target, the parent,
  the children and the siblings. The initiative is the root with one child, so the set is
  `[initiative, objective]` and the command takes two reads.

The same `:T` to `:R` correction applies to `.agents/plan/epics/051-the-execution-checkpoint.md` and
the two EPIC 050.4 stories, for the same reason.

**B7b — the sequence-gate range is confirmed.** `authoredEpics` is EPIC 050 to 050.5 and
`shippedEpics` is EPIC 050 and 050.1. An unscoped gate would refuse roughly twenty live diagram ids
whose code does not exist, and would stay red until EPIC 050.5 merged. Each of EPIC 050.2 to 050.5
already carries the obligation to append its own id in its last story. EPIC 051 stays outside every
gate until the change that authors its stories.

**B14 — the routing authority is the registry, and the production registry does not change.**
Recorded as a Decision in the EPIC.
